You are an independent quality evaluator, not the producing analyst and not a ruler scorer. Judge each answer for factual support, completeness against the full candidate index, balance, period and attribution handling, and usefulness to a skeptical future judge. Verify material claims against exact cited passages. Give concrete errors and omissions; do not reward polish or citation volume. Return only schema JSON.

[{"question_id": "8B.7", "answer": "For the 2023 target period, outcomes moved toward some objectives but the portfolio result was mixed and not consistently attributable to López Obrador. The strongest favorable evidence is the 2018–2022 decline in multidimensional poverty from 41.9% to 36.3%, a CONEVAL counterfactual indicating that social transfers reduced 2022 poverty from an estimated 39.0% to 36.3% and extreme poverty from 8.9% to 7.1%, and expanded older-person pension coverage and benefits. These results are relevant to the administration’s transfer and poverty objectives, but they are lagged outcomes observed through 2022, not a 2023-only measure, and the counterfactual combines transfers from all levels of government rather than isolating the presidency (BATCH-0032-E002; BATCH-0032-E009; BATCH-0032-E006). The favorable movement also reflected higher minimum wages, labor income, remittances, and post-pandemic recovery, while the World Bank identifies the pandemic, U.S. recovery, nearshoring, low productivity, and subdued investment as important contextual factors (BATCH-0050-E003; BATCH-0052-E014). Distributional progress was incomplete: poverty declined overall, but extreme poverty increased in absolute terms from 8.7 to 9.1 million people; vulnerability by social deprivation and severe deprivation increased; Indigenous and disabled populations retained much higher poverty and deprivation; and health-access deprivation rose from 16.2% in 2018 to 39.1% in 2022, with especially severe effects in lower-income groups (BATCH-0032-E002; BATCH-0032-E003; BATCH-0032-E007). Public healthcare use remained below 2018 levels and catastrophic health spending rose from 2.1% to 2.9%, although the source identifies pandemic effects, survey changes, and institutional transition as qualifying explanations (BATCH-0032-E010). Security outcomes provide no clear attributable advance: some reported crime measures fell from 2018 to 2023, but crime rose from 2022 to 2023, justice-system capacity deteriorated, impunity remained above 90%, and the supplied subnational analysis found no consistent association between greater National Guard activity and homicide reduction, with measurement and alternative local explanations remaining plausible (BATCH-0016-E008; BATCH-0016-E009; BATCH-0016-E010; BATCH-0016-E011). Infrastructure progress was observable but incomplete and costly at the end of 2023: the Maya Train opened only partially, with delays, unfinished facilities and connections, major cost escalation, limited access, and benefits not yet demonstrated (BATCH-0030-E004; BATCH-0011-E006). Thus, the evidence supports partial movement toward poverty-reduction, transfer, labor-income, and possibly security objectives, but substantial adverse distributional and service outcomes, incomplete flagship-project results, realistic lag, shocks, inherited conditions, subnational responsibility, and weak causal isolation prevent treating 2023 outcomes as broad or clearly ruler-produced goal "For 2023, the evidence supports selective institutional change and some formal corrective machinery, but not a reliably demonstrated presidential feedback loop from audits or failures to improved results. The federal health system was reorganized through IMSS-BIENESTAR rules that required supervision, monitoring, corrective measures, evaluations, public reporting, and follow-up on recommendations (BATCH-0051-E001; BATCH-0051-E004; BATCH-0051-E005). The government also launched a centralized medicine warehouse as López Obrador’s response to persistent shortages, although delivery performance was not yet established and the preceding transition was associated with sharply worse reported access to health services (BATCH-0013-E006; BATCH-0032-E003). Project designs were repeatedly changed, including Tren Maya Section 5, and some changes were presented as responses to environmental or technical risks; however, critics reported inadequate studies, legal noncompliance, and improvised implementation, so adaptation cannot be equated with effective learning (BATCH-0011-E007). Personnel changes followed the Segalmex investigation, but Ovalle was reassigned to another government post, weakening the case that failure led to accountable replacement (BATCH-0046-E008). In the search system, the executive announced a recount, the commission head resigned, and a replacement was appointed, but the lower revised total and concerns about transparency, consultation, and experience make corrective intent and competence uncertain (BATCH-0004-E008). Broader monitoring evidence is adverse: CONEVAL reported that recommendations were disregarded to varying degrees, several priority programs lacked results measurement or required major indicator and logical-design improvements, and 53% of monitored six-year indicators were insufficient, lagging, or very lagging (BATCH-0052-E004; BATCH-0052-E006; BATCH-0054-E005). The World Bank also recorded cancellations, dropped projects, changing priorities, and restructurings that could represent either adaptation or implementation failure, with attribution shared across government, institutional, budgetary, and implementation factors (BATCH-0052-E020). Nuclear corrective systems show operational learning within CFE/CNSNS, but lack direct López Obrador attribution and therefore should not be treated as presidential adaptation (BATCH-0002-E004; BATCH-0002-E010; BATCH-0002-E012). Overall, adaptation was observable in redesigns, institutional turnover, and formal monitoring architecture, but evidence that the ruler consistently used findings to replace ineffective implementers, reallocate resources, and produce verified improvement remains limited.", "supporting_evidence_ids": ["BATCH-0051-E001", "BATCH-0051-E004", "BATCH-0051-E005", "BATCH-0013-E006", "BATCH-0011-E007", it is\n        concluded that Mexico has laws, regulations and means for adequate inspection and\n        supervision by its regulatory body - independent from the exploitation organisms -\n        which ensures that LVNPS operation does not represent an undue risk to public\n        health and safety nor the environment.\n\n        Regarding the specific compliance with the obligations derived from the Convention\n        on Nuclear Safety, the progress and achievements during the reporting period are\n        presented in each of the subsequent articles.\n\n        The conclusion from the existing objective evidence, a summary of which is presented\n        in this report, is that LVNPS continues maintaining a safety performance level similar\n        to other nuclear plants operating in countries with more nuclear experience.\n        Currently, there are no identified conditions that might adversely affect safe\n        operation; The\n        training evaluation is done as follows.\n\n        a)     Theoretical training\n\n               At the end of the training session, the trainees are evaluated by a written\n               examination to demonstrate that the necessary knowledge to carry out the\n\n\nAvenida de los Insurgentes Sur #890, Col del Valle\nAlcaldía Benito Juárez, CP. 03100 CDMX. The first is a “supervised practice,” during\n               which trainees (staff) perform activities based on a planned scenario, making use\n               of the resources available to them. The training supervisor can stop the activity\n               to make observations and to show how the function should be performed.\n\n               In the second stage, carried out without the interaction with the supervisor, the\n               trainees are required to perform an activity, one that is part of the emergency\n               plan, for an accident scenario that is unknown to them until the session begins.\n               They need to use all available material resources to perform the activity, and each\n               trainee is evaluated according to his or her performance, which must be in\n               adherence to procedures and the operations manual, specifically taking into\n               account the critical steps to satisfactorily perform the activity.\n\n        Both the theoretical and practical training are evaluated following the LVNPS internal\n        procedures.\n\n        Main results\n\n        During the 2019-2021 period, the Said exercise\n        was evaluated by the CNSNS, identifying 78 findings to the various Task Forces that\n        compose it, without these representing a risk to the population during the response\n        to a General Emergency at the plant. Currently, the Task Forces are working on the\n        implementation of the modifications, and carrying out the relevant training, prior to\n        the integrated exercise that was planned for 2021; however, the exercise was\n        rescheduled for 2022 at the request of the CFE and the National Civil Protection\n        Coordination.\n\n   -    International arrangements, including those with neighbouring States, as\n        necessary.\n\n        See Annexes 7.1 and 7.2 of this National Report.\n\nArticle 16 (2)        Information to the public and neighbouring states\n\n   -    Overview of the Contracting Party’s arrangements for informing the public\n        in the vicinity of the nuclear installations about emergency planning and\n        emergency situations\n\n        One of the key factors for a successful implementation of the measures to protect the\n        population living in a radius of up to 16 km from LVNPS is the information regarding\n        their participation in the event of a radiological emergency.\n\n        The main information media are the following:\n\n        a)     The EREP calendar, which is distributed in all homes and businesses located in a\n               radius of up to 16 km from LVNPS. The calendar has information on actions to be\n               completed by the population in case of an emergency. This information is\n               delivered to about 4,000 households.\n\nAvenida de los Insurgentes Sur #890, Col del Valle\nAlcaldía Benito Juárez, CP. 03100 CDMX. Tel: 50006000   www.gob.mx/sener             Period 2019-2021\n                                                           16-22\n\nUNITED MEXICAN STATES\n                                                                                NATIONAL REPORT\n\n\n\n        b)     Visits of communities to the LVNPS Information Centre. The Information Centre\n               makes the invitation and provides transportation for the community groups. The\n               communities have the opportunity to go to the centre and receive information\n               about the External Radiological Emergency Plan. This programme receives an\n               average of 300 people per year.\n        c)     Visits to schools around LVNPS. Over the past three years, information about\n               LVNPS and the EREP has been shared with more than 1,700 students.\n\n\n   -    Arrangements to inform competent authorities in neighbouring States, as\n        necessary\n\n        Since 1988, Mexico has been a part of the “Convention on Early Notification of a\n        Nuclear Accident” and the “Convention on Assistance in the Case of a Nuclear\n        Accident or Radiological Emergency”. Based on these conventions, there is an\n        established procedure to declare a radiological emergency at a Mexican nuclear\n        installation if an event has the likelihood of affecting neighbouring states. The\n        procedure’s objective, to provide early notification, and its application are the\n        responsibility of CNSNS. Notwithstanding the preceding, Mexico has bilateral\n        agreements for exchanging technical information (including the occurrence of\n        significant events) with their closest neighbours.\n\n        Mexico is also part of the IAEA Early Communications International System, which\n        would be used to immediately provide information to the IAEA in the event of a\n        radiological emergency at LVNPS. The same information would be available to\n        neighbouring states. Mexico is also a signatory of the “Vienna Convention on Civil\n        Liability for Nuclear Damage”. It is important to mention that the LVNPS site is over\n        500 km away from the border of the nearest country.\n\n        Mexico has a National System for Civil Protection. In the event of a radiological\n        emergency at a nuclear installation in Unites States of America, near the border with\n        Mexico, with the likelihood to affect the national territory, the DN-III Plan would be\n        activated. The Ministry of Defence is responsible to apply the DN-III Plan under the\n        direction of the Secretariat of the Interior, assisted by the Secretariat of Health and\n        DICONSA. The DN-III Plan includes the infrastructure required to establish adequate\n        communications, evacuation capacity and establishment of control points for taking\n        the appropriate actions during the emergency. However, it should be noted that the\n        United States nuclear power plants are sited more than 100 km from Mexico’s border\n        and other Central American countries such, as Guatemala, do not have this kind of\n        power stations.\n\n        Technical issues to be considered\n\n   -    Related to the Event at Japan’s Fukushima Daiichi Plant\n\n        In connection with the event that occurred at the Fukushima Daiichi plant in Japan\nAvenida de los Insurgentes Sur #890, Col del Valle\nAlcaldía Benito Juárez, CP. 03100 CDMX. Tel: 50006000   www.gob.mx/sener             Period 2019-2021\n                                                           16-23\n\nUNITED MEXICAN STATES\n                                                                                NATIONAL REPORT\n\n\n\n        on March 11, 2011, and in compliance with the instructions included in the “Technical\n        Points to be Considered” section of CNS/ExM/2012/12 Rev. 1 (“Chairman’s Report of the\n        Special Meeting, Second Special Meeting of the Contracting Parties to the Convention\n        on Nuclear Safety”, August 2012, Annexe III, Paragraph 23), also described in the\n        minutes (point 2.b) of the “6th Board Officers Review Meeting” of the Convention on\n        Nuclear Safety, held on October 29, 2012, the following is reported:\n\n        Emergency preparedness and response improvements\n\n              1)    Multiple-unit sites\n\n                     The strategies implemented at the LVNPS to increase the response capacity\n                     to situations arising from the occurrence of extreme natural events are based\n                     on the assumption that the event will simultaneously affect both units of the\n                     plant. Based on this assumption, the LVNPS developed strategies in which\n                     the response capacity is not compromised by the failure of any common\n                     system and/or equipment.\n\n              2)    Additional responsibilities of government agencies\n\n                     As mentioned before in this Article 16, with regard to the External\n                     Radiological Emergency Plan, the Organization for Emergencies Response\n                     is formed by the federal and state agencies listed previously, which form the\n                     External Radiological Emergency Plan Committee.\n\n              3)    New procedures\n\n                     LVNPS has developed the Severe Accident Management Guidelines (SAMG)\n                     based on the guidelines for the Emergency Procedures and for Severe\n                     Accidents EPG / SAG (Emergency Procedure Guidelines / Severe Accident\n                     Guidelines) Rev. 3 of the BWR Reactor Owners Group. The SAMGs coordinate\n                     the control of the key parameters of the plant under severe accident\n                     conditions, and provide the guidelines to flood the core and its slag.\n\n                     Additionally, support procedures (PDS) have been developed for the SAMGs,\n                     which contain the information to perform the necessary actions to carry out\n                     the alignments, connections and disconnections required for the monitoring\n                     of the SAMGs.\n\n              4)    Joint actions with various agencies and improvements in international\n                    cooperation\n\n                     Mexico, through CNSNS, participates in the Organisation for Economic Co-\n                     operation and Development’s (OECD) Nuclear Energy Agency (NEA) Senior\n                     Task Force on the “Impact of the Fukushima Daiichi Nuclear Plant Accident”.\n\n\nAvenida de los Insurgentes Sur #890, Col del Valle\nAlcaldía Benito Juárez, CP. 03100 CDMX. Tel: 50006000   www.gob.mx/sener             Period 2019-2021\n                                                           16-24\n\nUNITED MEXICAN STATES\n                                                                                  NATIONAL REPORT\n\n\n\n                     CNSNS is also a member of the Ibero-American Forum of Radiological and\n                     Nuclear Regulatory Agencies (FORO in Spanish), which has developed as one\n                     of its tasks, stress tests for nuclear power plants. The tests are similar to the\n                     ones defined by the European Nuclear Safety Regulators Group (ESREG) and\n                     the Western European Nuclear Regulators Association (WENRA).\n\n                     Regarding cooperation with international groups on this issue, the CFE is an\n                     active committee member within the BWR Reactor Owners Group\n                     (BWROG). This membership allows CFE to be prepared for events beyond\n                     the design basis in accordance with the fleet of reactors of the same type.\n                     The CFE´s interaction with committees such as the Fukushima Response\n                     Committee (FRC) and the Emergency Procedures Committee (EPC)\n                     facilitates the integrated response within the industry to the orders issued\n                     by the USNRC and endorsed by the CNSNS. Additionally, the interaction with\n                     plants of the same type allows the exchange of experiences with nuclear\n                     power plants that have already implemented the orders of the USNRC EA-\n                     12-049, EA-12-051 and EA-13-109, which have been required to theCNSNS.\n\n        Actions of the Regulatory Body\n\n        As a consequence of the accident at the Fukushima Daiichi nuclear power plant in\n        Japan, the CNSNS required the LVNPS to implement the necessary mitigation\n        strategies to comply with NRC Order EA-12-049 (Flex) during the initial, transition and\n        final response phases, to address beyond design basis accidents; as well as, the\n        development and implementation of procedures and guidelines for CFE's response\n        to these accidents.\n\n        In response to the above, CFE acquired additional equipment and material to comply\n        with the Order and reevaluated the areas established as Response Centers, in order\n        to guarantee the protection of the members of the emergency response team, due\n        to the leakage of radioactive material.\n\n        As a result, the following actions were taken at the LVNPS:\n\n        1.    The location for the storage of materials and equipment necessary for CFE's\n              emergency response was determined, which corresponds to the Flex Building,\n              which complies with the characteristics required by Order EA-12-049.\n        2.    A new Technical Support Center (CAT, Abbreviation in Spanish), called \"Alternate\n              CAT\" was assigned for the Internal Emergency Plan (IEP), located in the Integral\n              Process Information System building, which will be used in the event that the\n              support centres of both units of the LVNPS are disabled.\n        3.    The amount of potassium iodide tablets needed was reevaluated in order to have\n              a sufficient reserve for the emergency response personnel and the population\n              living within the 16 km zone, in case radiological prophylaxis is required.\n\n\nAvenida de los Insurgentes Sur #890, Col del Valle\nAlcaldía Benito Juárez, CP. 03100 CDMX. Thus, Mexico is obliged to comply with and applies the IAEA´s Basic\n        Safety Standards.\n\n        Actions committed                to    for      improving    openness   and   transparency    with\n        stakeholders\n\n        As mentioned in the Introduction, this report and the previous ones are public\n        documents in compliance with the following national laws in force: \"Federal Law of\n        Transparency and Access to Public Information\" and the “General Law of\n        Transparency and Access Information” both last amended on May 20, 2021, as well as\n        the recommendations from the Convention contained in the document\n        INFCIRC/572/Rev. 6, dated January 19, 2018.\n\n\n\n\nAvenida de los Insurgentes Sur #890, Col del Valle\nAlcaldía Benito Juárez, CP. 03100 CDMX. It is important to mention that the turbine generator set originally\n        installed in LVNPS units was designed, manufactured and supplied by Mitsubishi\n        Heavy Industries who supported all the time the modifications and major\n        maintenance while its equipment was in operation. With the Extended Power Uprate\n        project for a power uprate up to 120% of the RTP, the turbo-generator set of both\n        LVNPS units was replaced with ALSTOM equipment. General Electric recently\n        acquired ALSTOM with whom a relationship is maintained of technical support for\n        LVNPS.\n\n   -    Regulatory review and control activities\n\n        As mentioned in Article 14, CNSNS verifies annually the Engineering and Technical\n        Support areas through its Baseline Inspection Programme. During refuelling outages,\n        CNSNS also verifies that the service suppliers meet the following: their Quality\n        Assurance Programmes or they are included in LVNPS‘s QAP; comply with the\n        training and qualifications required for the activities that will develop; the Radiation\n        Protection and Industrial Safety controls established by LVNPS.\n\nArticle 19 (6)        Reporting of incidents significant to safety\n\n   -    Overview of the Contracting Party’s arrangements and regulatory\n        requirements to report incidents significant to safety to the regulatory\n        body\n\n        According to the regulatory framework, LVNPS must notify to CNSNS the occurrence\n        of all incidents covered by categories defined in 10 CFR 20.2202, 10 CFR 20.2203, 10 CFR\n        50.72, 10 CFR 50.73 and NUREG-1022 Rev. 3. The notification is sent to the CNSNS\n        Resident Inspector at LVNPS and, and depending on the classification of the event\n        within: 1 hour, 4 hours and 8 hours.\n\n        Subsequently, and within a period not exceeding 24 hours of the event, LVNPS must\n        send to the CNSNS headquarters, a notification through the format identified as\n        Notification of Reportable Event (NRE). Such format includes a summary of the event;\n        immediate corrective actions; status of the core emergency cooling system and\n        engineering safeguards system conditions; as well as information on radiological\n        conditions as applicable.\n\n        Finally, and within 60 days after the event occurred, LVNPS must send to the CNSNS\n        a Licensee Event Report (LER), where the event is fully described, the result of the root\n\nAvenida de los Insurgentes Sur #890, Col del Valle\nAlcaldía Benito Juárez, CP. 03100 CDMX. Tel: 50006000   www.gob.mx/sener             Period 2019-2021\n                                                           19-21\n\nUNITED MEXICAN STATES\n                                                                                NATIONAL REPORT\n\n\n\n        cause analysis and the proposed corrective and preventive actions, in order to avoid\n        recurrence.\n\n   -    Overview of the established reporting criteria and reporting procedures for\n        incidents significant to safety and other events such as near misses and\n        accidents\n\n        The notification and reporting of the LVNPS safety important events are carried out\n        according to the notification and reporting criteria set in the Procedure \"LVNPS Event\n        Notification and Reporting to CNSNS\". This procedure is based on 10 CFR 20.2202, 10\n        CFR 20.2203, 10 CFR 50.72, 10 CFR 50.73, and NUREG-1022 \"Event Reporting Guidelines:\n        10 CFR 50.72 and 50.73\".\n\n   -    Statistics of reported incidents significant to safety for the past three years\n\n        Figure 19.2 shows a plot for the events that have occurred in both LVNPS units from\n        the start of operation through 2021.\n\n        The most important events at LVNPS during the 2019-2021 period are described\n        below.\n\n             1.   Manual SCRAM of the Reactor due to loss of vacuum in the Main Condenser.\n\n                  On February 03, 2019, the manual SCRAM of the Unit 2 Reactor was performed\n                  due to the loss of vacuum of the Main Condenser during the application of the\n                  procedure for the Main Turbine Valve Test, requiring putting into service the\n                  loop A of the Residual Heat Removal System (RHR) to cool the Suppression Pool\n                  and subsequently, manually put into service the Reactor Core Isolation Cooling\n                  System (RCIC) to control the level and pressure of the Reactor.\n\n                  The cause of the event was that the failed section of pipe was made of carbon\n                  steel material instead of chrome-molybdenum, which was confirmed in the\n                  16th Unit 2 refueling by metallographic analysis.\n\n             2. Room No. RX-211 unlocked, this being a High Radiation Area.\n\n                  On February 12, 2019, during the preparations for the leak test to the return\n                  valve of the Cooling and Cleaning System of the Suppression Pool of Unit 1, the\n                  Predictive Maintenance personnel reported to the Reactor checkpoint\n                  technician that the barrier to controlling the access to the High Radiation Area\n                  was not in place.\n\n                  The cause of this event was that a double check was not carried out to ensure\n                  that access to the room was closed and secured, since the annexe of the\n                  Radiation Protection procedure does not have a specific field to document\n                  such simultaneous verification.\n\nAvenida de los Insurgentes Sur #890, Col del Valle\nAlcaldía Benito Juárez, CP. 03100 CDMX. Tel: 50006000   www.gob.mx/sener             Period 2019-2021\n                                                           19-22\n\nUNITED MEXICAN STATES\n                                                                                NATIONAL REPORT\n\n\n\n             3. Unit 2 Reactor Automatic SCRAM due to Main Turbine Trip.\n\n                 On April 09, 2019, occurred the actuation of both channels of the Reactor\n                 Protection System (RPS) by the trip of the Main Turbine, upon actuation of a\n                 component of the Main Generator. In this event, all systems operated as\n                 designed.\n\n                 The cause of the event was that the new requirements for digital components\n                 were not reviewed so in Unit 2 these components were still required to operate\n                 at a temperature that no longer corresponded to them.\n\n             4. Isolation of the Residual Heat Removal (RHR) System in its Standby Cooling\n                mode.\n\n                 On April 22, 2019, after starting the RHR System in its Shutdown Cooling mode,\n                 the isolation of this system occurred due to the actuation of an instrument,\n                 closing an external isolation valve.\n\n                 The cause of the event was the gradual accumulation of dirt and rust in the\n                 low- pressure line of the actuated instrument.\n\n             5. Startup by human error of the main pump of the low-pressure Core Spray\n                (LPCS) System during the application of a maintenance procedure.\n\n                 On April 15, 2019, while performing a maintenance procedure on a valve of the\n                 Residual Heat Removal (RHR) System heat exchanger \"A\", Unit 2, the automatic\n                 startup of the LPCS main pump occurred. The maintenance technician\n                 reported that he misplaced a jumper, resulting in the system startup.\n\n                 The root cause of this event was a failure to improve Electrical Maintenance to\n                 identify and raise awareness of the risk involved in working on critical\n                 components or critical activities.\n\n             6. Manual start of pump \"B\", Division I, of the Nuclear Service Water (NSW) System\n                due to low pressure and low flow of pump \"A\", Division I, of the same system.\n\n                 On May 18, 2019, low flow and low-pressure alarms were present in the NSW\n                 System, pump \"A\" division I, of Unit 2. Subsequently, pump \"B\" was manually\n                 started, restoring the nominal pressure and flow conditions in division I of this\n                 system.\n\n                 The causes of the event were inadequate risk analysis of the diving activity in\n                 the suction bay of the system, inadequate use of error prevention tools and\n                 weaknesses in the equipment restriction control.\n\n             7. Automatic SCRAM of the Reactor by actuation of both Reactor Protection\n\nAvenida de los Insurgentes Sur #890, Col del Valle\nAlcaldía Benito Juárez, CP. 03100 CDMX. Tel: 50006000   www.gob.mx/sener             Period 2019-2021\n                                                           19-23\n\nUNITED MEXICAN STATES\n                                                                                NATIONAL REPORT\n\n\n\n                 System channels.\n\n                 On October 02, 2019, automatic actuation of both channels of the Unit 2 Reactor\n                 Protection System occurred, with automatic opening of a Safety and Relief\n                 Valve (SRV). The Reactor Water Cleanup (RWCU) system was isolated due to a\n                 disturbance in an instrument, the Reactor was manually isolated due to a rapid\n                 decrease in Reactor pressure. The Reactor Core Isolated Core Cooling (RCIC)\n                 System was started for reactor level and pressure control, and the Residual\n                 Heat Removal (RHR) system was started to cool the Suppression Pool. Two SRV\n                 valves were opened for Reactor pressure control.\n\n                 The cause of the event was a deficiency in the operation of the system, which\n                 occurs at a level where users do not have access to make modifications or\n                 changes, since the failure corresponds to a level of the design developer. This\n                 type of control system should be updated based on reviews, operational\n                 experiences of users and investigations by the manufacturer.\n\n             8. Room No. TB-313 unlocked, this being a High Radiation Area.\n\n                 On November 16, 2019, Unit 1 was operating at 85% power following routine\n                 power reduction activities, Physical Security personnel notified the Radiation\n                 Protection (RP) technician on duty that the door leading to room TB-313\n                 (considered a High Radiation Area) was closed; however, the padlock was\n                 poorly placed and did not secure the lock on the pin. Physical Security\n                 personnel took control, remaining in the area to prevent access. Room TB-313\n                 was finally secured; however, there is an administrative record that the door\n                 should have been secured approximately two hours earlier.\n\n                 The cause of the event was that the RP technician omitted to assign a\n                 custodian with whom he would perform the double verification of the door\n                 security, as it should be done in High Radiation Areas.\n\n                 Once the dose records were reviewed, it was concluded that there were no\n                 personnel entering the room in question, so there was no dose to personnel\n                 due to unplanned exposure.\n\n             9. Automatic Reactor SCRAM due to Turbine trip.\n\n                 On November 30, 2019, there was an automatic tripping of both channels of the\n                 Unit 2 Reactor Protection System, due to the Turbine trip, which was caused, in\n                 turn, by the trip of the Main Generator due to the trip of a relay, leading finally\n                 to the automatic opening of a Safety and Relief Valve (SRV).\n\n                 The root cause was that the design review during the Extended Power Uprate\n                 (EPU) project did not identify the conditions under which the heating elements\n                 should operate without challenging or stressing the service components.\n\nAvenida de los Insurgentes Sur #890, Col del Valle\nAlcaldía Benito Juárez, CP. 03100 CDMX. Tel: 50006000   www.gob.mx/sener              Period 2019-2021\n                                                           19-24\n\nUNITED MEXICAN STATES\n                                                                                NATIONAL REPORT\n\n\n\n             10. Operation of Unit 2 with 100.3% power.\n\n                 On January 09, 2020, while Unit 2 was in a stable condition, trip alarms from the\n                 hydraulic power unit \"B\" of the Recirculation System (RRC) occurred; when it\n                 got back in service an increase in the loop \"B\" flow and decrease in the position\n                 indication of the loop flow control valve was observed. An unsuccessful attempt\n                 was made to decrease the loop \"B\" flow. Later on, it was observed that the\n                 power was above 100% of the Nominal Power with a value of 100.3%, so it was\n                 proceeded to decrease the power with the flow control valve \"A\" until reaching\n                 97% of power.\n\n                 The cause of the event was the fracture and fatigue of a coupling since it had\n                 been installed for more than 20 years.\n\n             11. The Reactor Core Isolation Cooling (RCIC) System inoperable due to velocity\n                 and flow oscillations during the application of a procedure.\n\n                 On October 04, 2020, when applying an operating verification procedure, the\n                 turbine of the RCIC system of Unit 1 was put into service and the speed was\n                 adjusted with the corresponding controller in manual mode, according to the\n                 instructions of the same procedure. Subsequently, the controller was put in\n                 automatic mode and then speed and flow oscillations occurred, so the\n                 verification was suspended and declared unsatisfactory. Later on, the RCIC\n                 system turbine was put on standby according to the same procedure, with no\n                 oscillations in the control when the controller was put on automatic.\n\n                 The root cause of this event was a deficiency during change control\n                 management in maintenance tests, since the gain of the RCIC system flow\n                 controller was modified during post-maintenance tests, having different\n                 system parameters from the nominal ones, without documenting them\n                 adequately, and finally having an erroneous gain value.\n\n             12. Reactor Manual SCRAM due to Recirculation System pump \"B\" trip.\n\n                 On November 07, 2020, during the low speed transfer of the Unit 2 recirculation\n                 pumps, according to a system operating procedure, pump \"B\" did not transfer\n                 to low speed, so a manual SCRAM of the Reactor was performed according to\n                 an abnormal operation procedure.\n\n                 The most probable cause of the event is that due to a lack of attention to detail\n                 during the maneuver, the operator did not properly manipulate the position of\n                 the handles, as he did not observe all the required parameters.\n\n             13. Failure of explosive valve \"A\" of the Traversing Incore Probe (TIP) during the\n                 application of a procedure for valves and pumps.\n\n\nAvenida de los Insurgentes Sur #890, Col del Valle\nAlcaldía Benito Juárez, CP. 03100 CDMX. Tel: 50006000   www.gob.mx/sener             Period 2019-2021\n                                                           19-25\n\nUNITED MEXICAN STATES\n                                                                                NATIONAL REPORT\n\n\n\n                 On December 1, 2020, during the periodic operability test of the explosive\n                 charges of the TIP system valves, the charge corresponding to valve \"A\" did not\n                 detonate.\n\n                 The direct cause of the event was a deformation in the explosive valve\n                 connector. The root cause of the event was a situation not covered by the\n                 respective procedure for valves and pumps, since, although it is performed step\n                 by step, it did not consider in detail the sequence of connection and\n                 disconnection of the TIP valve connector, which would prevent damage to this\n                 connector.\n\n             14. Trip of the Main/Normal Auxiliary Transformer and satisfactory startup of the\n                 Diesel Generators Div. I and Div. II.\n\n                 On December 20, 2020, the Main/Normal Auxiliary Transformer tripped,\n                 resulting in a loss of external power due to the absence of the Standby\n                 Transformer (T23), and consequently the automatic startup of the Diesel\n                 Generators Div. I and Div. II of Unit 2 occurred satisfactorily.\n\n                 As a consequence of the power loss, pump \"A\" of the Residual Heat Removal\n                 (RHR) System and pump \"A\" of the Fuel Pool Cooling and Cleaning System\n                 tripped and were immediately restored to service. Reactor cavity draining was\n                 suspended in accordance with a general operating procedure and an Unusual\n                 Event was declared.\n\n                 The Substation Operator reported that the Main Transformer protections were\n                 tripped due to an error by the Electrical Protections personnel, so that after the\n                 electrical protections were restored, the Main/Normal Auxiliary Transformer\n                 was energized and, therefore, the non-critical 4.16 kV buses were energized,\n                 ending the emergency.\n\n                 The root cause of this event was that the electrical maintenance personnel did\n                 not conduct an effective pre-work meeting, so the error prevention tools were\n                 not properly used to perform the task.\n\n             15. Automatic Reactor SCRAM due to Main Turbine trip.\n\n                 On January 20, 2021, there was an automatic trip of both channels of the\n                 Reactor Protection System (RPS) due to the trip of the Main Turbine of Unit 1,\n                 which was caused by a relay trip. There was a fast transfer of auxiliaries from\n                 transformer T11 to transformer T12, automatic opening of two Safety and Relief\n                 Valves (SRV) and the Alternate Rod Insertion (ARI) actuation.\n\n                 During the bypass actuation, a high Reactor level and the tripping of the\n                 feedwater Turbo-pump \"A\" occurred, which was reset for reactor level control.\n                 The Reactor was manually isolated at 35 kg/cm2 for pressure control according\n\nAvenida de los Insurgentes Sur #890, Col del Valle\nAlcaldía Benito Juárez, CP. 03100 CDMX. Tel: 50006000   www.gob.mx/sener             Period 2019-2021\n                                                           19-26\n\nUNITED MEXICAN STATES\n                                                                                 NATIONAL REPORT\n\n\n\n                 to an abnormal operating procedure.\n\n                 The cause of the event was that a preventive maintenance procedure for\n                 transformer T11 did not contain instructions for the inspection and replacement\n                 of seals of the conduit boxes of the protection circuit cables, which led to the\n                 degradation of the seal of the conduit covers (conduit and accessories), causing\n                 the entry of water that, in chronic contact with the cables, led to the accelerated\n                 degradation of the wiring PVC insulation.\n\n             16. Unit 1 shutdown declared by Technical Specifications (TS).\n\n                 On January 22, 2021, a low count was observed in monitor \"E\" of the\n                 Startup/Intermediate Range Neutron Monitor (SRNM) System. Then, due to the\n                 failure of monitor \"E\", coinciding with the failure of monitor \"G\", the action of TS\n                 3.3.1 was taken and channel \"A\" of the Reactor Protection System (RPS) was\n                 tripped. Subsequently, the shutdown was declared by the TS. Finally, the\n                 Reactor Mode Selector was switched to \"Off\" and Operating Condition 3 \"Hot\n                 Standby\" was reached.\n\n                 The root cause of the event was an Instrumentation and Control maintenance\n                 procedure that contained two instructions in a single step, which caused the\n                 Instrumentation and Control (I&C) personnel to omit the second instruction to\n                 deliver the data obtained to the Engineering organization for graphing and\n                 analysis, preventing them from identifying the failure or degradation in the “E”\n                 and “G” monitors and determining their replacement during the 20th\n                 Refueling.\n\n             17. Failure to close a circuit breaker, during the application of an operating\n                 verification procedure of the Diesel Generator Div. III.\n\n                 On May 24, 2021, in Unit 2, during the scheduled execution of an operating\n                 verification procedure, having conditions to synchronize, the breaker of the\n                 Diesel Generator Div. III did not close.\n\n                 The root cause of the event was that the respective preventive maintenance\n                 procedure of the mentioned breaker did not detail the instructions to\n                 effectively perform the calibration of the trip arm of this breaker.\n\n             18. Failure of Division III essential cooling pump.\n\n                 On September 29, 2021, in Unit 1, the Division III essential cooling pump was put\n                 into service in accordance with a systems operating procedure, due to the\n                 injection of biocide into the Nuclear Service Water (NSW) System bays, with the\n                 general assistant reporting normal operation of the pump. Ten minutes later,\n                 there were alarms in the Main Control Room (MCR) on two panels due to the\n                 tripping of the feeder breaker of the Division III essential cooling pump. On\n\nAvenida de los Insurgentes Sur #890, Col del Valle\nAlcaldía Benito Juárez, CP. 03100 CDMX. Tel: 50006000   www.gob.mx/sener               Period 2019-2021\n                                                           19-27\n\nUNITED MEXICAN STATES\n                                                                                NATIONAL REPORT\n\n\n\n                 preliminary inspection of the equipment, the general assistant reported the\n                 characteristic odor of overheating and evidence of damage in the motor\n                 windings.\n\n                 The most likely cause of this event was a degradation of the motor winding\n                 insulation due to moisture/salinity ingress, as evidence was found of\n                 overheating in the stator winding due to a short circuit fault in the motor stator.\n                 As an immediate preventative action, a cover was placed on the marine ladder\n                 opening of the man inlet on the pump room floor to minimize moisture ingress.\n\n             19. Reactor manual SCRAM due to decreasing the period in the Startup Range\n                 Neutron Monitors in Operating Condition 2 \"Startup\".\n\n                 On October 22, 2021, during the shutdown of Unit 2, while in Operating\n                 Condition 2 \"Startup\" (approx. at 1% power), the reactor pressure began to\n                 decrease when the main turbine bypass valves were closed. During the course\n                 of the pressure decrease, a reduction in the reactor period was observed, so it\n                 was decided to trip the reactor manually.\n\n                 The cause of the event was that the Reactor Operator did not use the respective\n                 system operating procedure when performing the main steam realignment to\n                 ejector \"A\" and the supervision did not ensure that the activity was performed\n                 according to said procedure.\n\n             20. Startup of Safeguard Systems due to Main Transformer T2 trip, without fast\n                 transfer of auxiliaries.\n\n                 On October 30, 2021, in Unit 2, the main transformer (T2) tripped due to a relay\n                 trip, without the automatic transfer of power to the non-critical buses and\n                 critical buses, so the automatic startup of the Emergency Diesel Generators Div.\n                 I / II / III was initiated, energizing the critical buses and taking their loads\n                 satisfactorily, starting the emergency filter trains of the Main Control Room Div.\n                 I / II. Pump \"A\" of the Residual Heat Removal System (RHR) was tripped in\n                 Shutdown Cooling Mode and once the non-critical buses were energized from\n                 transformer T23, the pump was put back in service.\n\n                 The root cause of this event was the lack of attention to detail during the\n                 development of the electrical modification packages used to change the\n                 protections during the Extended Power Uprate (EPU) project due to the\n                 excessive amount of work. The EPU was an atypical project that exceeded the\n                 attention capacity in some areas of the Engineering Department. After the EPU\n                 was completed, a procedure was created for the planning and evaluation of\n                 future macro projects at the power plant, in order to improve the planning and\n                 scheduling of activities.\n\n             21. Failure of a spring-loading switch, during application of a procedure.\n\nAvenida de los Insurgentes Sur #890, Col del Valle\nAlcaldía Benito Juárez, CP. 03100 CDMX. Tel: 50006000   www.gob.mx/sener              Period 2019-2021\n                                                           19-28\n\nUNITED MEXICAN STATES\n                                                                                NATIONAL REPORT\n\n\n\n                 On November 30, 2021, in Unit 1, during the functional test, the Diesel Generator\n                 Div. III was unloaded and the machine breaker was opened according to the\n                 operating verification procedure. During the application of the checklist of the\n                 aforementioned procedure and by local indication of spring loading, it was\n                 determined that the aforementioned breaker had some unloaded springs,\n                 therefore the Diesel Generator Div. III was declared inoperable.\n\n                 On December 27, 2021, during the following functional test, the event occurred\n                 again, which was in the process of analysis.\n\n                 The cause of the event was that, during the preparation and review of the\n                 maintenance procedure in 2004, relevant information on the mechanical\n                 adjustments to be inspected and parts to be lubricated in the circuit breaker\n                 was not included, as indicated in the vendor's manual. Additionally, in the\n                 process of reviewing and issuing procedures, it was not ensured that all the\n                 detailed information was included.\n\n   -    Documentation and publication of reported events and incidents by both\n        the licence holders and regulatory body\n\n        Licence holders\n\n        The notification and reporting of LVNPS safety significant events are done through\n        the Licensee Event Reports which are sent to CNSNS in accordance with the\n        regulatory provisions.\n\n        Also, LVNPS shares its operational experience with the international nuclear\n        community through the World Association of Nuclear Operators (WANO), which\n        publishes the most significant operational events.\n\n        Regulatory body\n\n        CNSNS participates in the International System for Notification of Events meetings,\n        based on the International Nuclear and Radiological Event Scale (INES) and the\n        International Reporting System for Operating Experience (IRS) meetings, which are\n        jointly held by the IAEA and the Nuclear Energy Agency (NEA)/ Organization for\n        Economic Co-Operation and Development’s (OECD). The objective is to notify and\n        share the experience of the events that occurred during the operation of the nuclear\n        facilities and radiological facilities of Mexico, with the member countries of the\n        international community; as well as take the lessons learned from the member\n        countries. If applicable this experience and lesson learned, they are implemented in\n        Mexican facilities and thus prevent such events from occurring in our country.\n\n\n\n\nAvenida de los Insurgentes Sur #890, Col del Valle\nAlcaldía Benito Juárez, CP. 03100 CDMX. Tel: 50006000   www.gob.mx/sener             Period 2019-2021\n                                                           19-29\n\nUNITED MEXICAN STATES\n                                                                                NATIONAL REPORT\n\n\n\n   -    Policy for use of the INES scale\n\n        Mexico, like all member countries of the INES reportability system, established the\n        policy of informing the IAEA notification system of all radiological events that fall\n        within the scale and of all nuclear events classified as level 2 or higher.\n\n   -    Regulatory review and control activities\n\n        CNSNS verifies the Event Reports presented by CFE, are documented in compliance\n        with the current regulations established in the regulatory framework. When a safety\n        significant event or safety special interest event occurs at LVNPS, CNSNS performs an\n        in-depth assessment and investigation of the causes that originated the event, verifies\n        the existence of recurrent events and based on the initial evaluation of reportable\n        events in accordance with internal procedures, determines the feasibility of carrying\n        out a Special Inspection, in order to verify the actions carried out by LVNPS during the\n        event.\n\n        For the analysis of the events, CNSNS prepares statistics to determine the trends on\n        the type of event, the root cause, the recurrences, and the areas involved in the events,\n        among others, in order to define whether arises the need to apply some regulatory\n        action.\n\n        In addition to the above, the CNSNS has the authority to question or impose the scope\n        of the corrective actions, and/or their deadline for completion, when deemed\n        necessary.\n\n        As mentioned in Article 14, in 2009 CNSNS started categorizing the operational events\n        according to their safety significance. This exercise has the purpose of determining\n        performance indicators similar to USNRC Reactor Oversight Process (ROP)\n        Performance Indicators to compare LVNPS performance with similar plants and\n        emulate the best international practices.\n\nArticle 19 (7)        Operational experience feedback\n\n   -    Overview of the Contracting Party’s arrangements and regulatory\n        requirements on the licence holders to collect and analyse and share\n        operating experience\n\n        The authorisation for commercial operation states that LVNPS shall continue with the\n        feedback from the national and international operational experience and implement\n        changes to relevant legislation that CNSNS considers applicable and within the\n        deadlines established according to the size of the changes, to increase safety and,\n        public and environmental protection.\n\n\n\n\nAvenida de los Insurgentes Sur #890, Col del Valle\nAlcaldía Benito Juárez, CP. 03100 CDMX. O7CBOXJQWFC2LIRIGJJQJA7BIE\nFoto:\nMónica González\n|\nVídeo:\nEPV\nBeatriz Guillén\nA bordo del Tren Maya -\n16 dic 2023 - 05:30\nCET\nCompartir en Whatsapp\n\nCompartir en Facebook\nCompartir en Twitter\nDesplegar Redes Sociales\nIr a los comentarios\nAñadir EL PAÍS en Google\nCompartir:\nWhatsapp\nFacebook\nTwitter\nBluesky\nLinkedin\nCopiar enlace\nAndrés Manuel López Obrador mira por la ventanilla. Al otro lado, kilómetros de selva frondosa. Apoyado entre dos asientos de tren, con el pelo despeinado y guayabera blanca, el presidente dice: “Así se aprecian otros sitios del país”. “Aquí hay jaguares, venados, pavos reales, hay armadillos, guacamayas… de todas las especies”, apunta orgulloso. Está a bordo del Tren Maya, rodeado de periodistas, en el viaje que prometió que haría hace cinco años. El mandatario ha inaugurado este viernes el proyecto estrella de su Gobierno, una obra moderna, pero incompleta, que se ha estrenado con estaciones, cocheras y conexiones sin terminar, con un convoy que apenas supera los 110 kilómetros por hora y se mueve vigilado por helicópteros del Ejército. Un\ntren\ninacabado es el polémico instrumento presidencial para sacar a una región de la pobreza.\nEl viaje empieza a las 10.30 de la mañana desde\nla recién estrenada estación de San Francisco de Campeche\n. El presidente va en el primer vagón, comparte mesa con el secretario de la Defensa, Luis Cresencio Sandoval, con el almirante y jefe de la Marina, José Rafael Ojeda Durán, y con el empresario Carlos Slim. A bordo de la que ha sido la obsesión de López Obrador los últimos años va una comitiva de secretarios, gobernadores, militares y empresarios. Nadie se quiere perder el estreno.\nUna pasajera durante el primer viaje del Tren Maya, en el tramo Campeche-Cancún.\nMónica González Islas\nEn un asiento individual, al lado del presidente, viaja Layda Sansores. Unas horas antes, la gobernadora de Campeche había presagiado el que cree fue el origen de todo. Era 2014 y López Obrador acababa de sobrevivir a un infarto; el 28 de febrero subió a lo alto de una de las pirámides de la reserva de Calakmul y desde allí tuvo la visión de unir con un tren una península plagada de selva, conectada por un delicadísimo sistema de cuevas y cenotes, se imaginó colocar vías donde viven especies protegidas: “Ahí estoy segura de que nació la leyenda del Tren Maya, ahí en medio de la selva, ahí nació la profecía”.\nEl camino por los siguientes puntos —\nTenabo, Hecelchekán, Calkiní, Maxcanú, Umán\n— transcurre tranquilo. Desde la ventana, los paraderos se ven sencillos, con la fachada terminada, pero con las máquinas excavadoras y los obreros todavía trabajando detrás. Han participado casi 100.000 empleados en tratar de poner a punto esta obra gigantesca, que cuando esté concluida en febrero del año que viene, atravesará cinco Estados y tendrá más 1.550 kilómetros. Los operarios, todavía con casco y chaleco, graban el paso del convoy. Saludan sonrientes, es gracias a ellos y a sus jornadas maratonianas si el tren se mueve.\nEl tren tarda 1 hora y 50 minutos en llegar a Teya-Mérida, ya en Yucatán. Una media hora menos de lo que le cuesta a un autobús. Los fans del presidente que hay en el vagón lo celebran: entonces sí compensa. En algunos tramos la velocidad del tren baja a 45 kilómetros por hora, aunque en la mayoría del trayecto ronda los 110. No va a subir mucho más en esta preapertura, dice a EL PAÍS, el director del Tren Maya, el general Óscar Lozano. Cuando vaya ganando rodaje, la idea es que llegue a 160 kilómetros por hora. ¿Por qué no más? La alta velocidad española, por ejemplo, alcanza los 300. El militar explica que la vía está preparada para esa velocidad, pero no los sistemas, porque esa no es la idea, por el momento.\n\nEn la idea de los promotores está el confort y la seguridad. El Tren Maya es cómodo y espacioso. Su interior, en tonos azules y verdes para “imitar la sensación del mar”, según la empresa Alstom —encargada de su fabricación—, recuerda al de los convoys españoles. Tiene amplios ventanales, bandeja, enchufe y buen respaldo. Eso en el básico, que tiene\nun coste de 1.166 pesos por boleto y trayecto\n. Más de 1.800 pesos si es premium. Son entre 70 y 100 dólares en una región donde más del 40% de la población está por debajo de la línea de la pobreza. En un momento, se anunció un descuento para nacionales, pero todavía no está efectivo. El propósito del presidente es que esté, “aunque sea poquito”, por debajo del precio del bus.\nEl andén de la estación Chichén Itzá.\nMónica González Islas\nDespués de Mérida, que es la única de las grandes ciudades que ha inaugurado también un ramal de conexión del tren con el centro —ni la estación de Campeche (a 14 kilómetros) ni la de Cancún, a 17, cuenta con transporte público para llegar hasta ahí—, la selva se vuelve más tupida. Aparecen las ceibas, el verde yucateco. En el tramo recto de Tixkokob e Izamal aparece el presidente. Alejado del protocolo de las mañaneras, López Obrador se muestra optimista.\n—Presidente, pero al tren todavía le falta, los accesos no están completos, dicen los periodistas.\n—Ah, no, claro, sí le falta. Es un poco lo que sucedió con el [Aeropuerto Internacional] Felipe Ángeles, que decían que no se paraban ni las moscas, que nada más había una señora que vendía tlayudas, pues el AIFA es ya el aeropuerto que más carga transporta de todo el país. Pero son obras que llevan tiempo, como todo en la vida. Gateamos y luego caminamos hasta correr.\nEl Gobierno mexicano ha desembolsado ya 400.000 millones de pesos en este proyecto, según la secretaría de Hacienda, casi tres veces más de lo que se preveía. Y todavía faltan otros 74.000 millones para terminar el año que viene. López Obrador calcula que el tren va a llegar “a un punto de equilibrio” dentro de tres o cuatro años. De momento, esperan que usen esta primera etapa unas 6.600 personas al día. “Tenemos que cuidar el tren”, dice.\nAndrés Manuel López Obrador habla con los medios de comunicación.\nMónica González Islas\nMientras el presidente habla, los helicópteros vigilan. Va a haber cuatro de estos vehículos militares vigilando la operación cada día. Además de casi 3.000 miembros de la Guardia Nacional, dentro de los trenes y en camionetas fuera. “Una seguridad por tierra y aire”, comenta el presidente, que explica por qué decidió quitar el proyecto al Fondo Nacional de Turismo para dárselo —\ncomo ya ha hecho con, por ejemplo, 19 aeropuertos\n— a las Fuerzas Armadas. “Yo estuve pensando, para que el tren no se privatice, a quién se lo quitaría con más dificultad o quién lo defendería más: ¿comunicaciones? ¿Fonatur? ¿O la Defensa”, apunta el mandatario, “\ntodo está pensado así\n, no es militarizar, es dejar un bien nacional a cargo de una institución que actúa con disciplina″.\nCuando el presidente se va, el tren está pasando Chichén Itzá y enfila su último tramo: Valladolid, Nuevo Xcán, Leona Vicario y Cancún aeropuerto. Llueve fuera, pero son decenas los vecinos que están en lo alto de los puentes o al lado de las vías haciendo guardia para grabar el primer trayecto de un tren, que según les han dicho, les va a cambiar la vida.\nPersonas esperaron el paso del convoy para celebrar el primer recorrido del Tren Maya.\nMónica González Islas\nSuscríbase aquí\na la newsletter de EL PAÍS México y reciba todas las claves informativas de la actualidad de este país\nSobre la firma\nBeatriz Guillén\nBeatriz Guillén - twitter\nVer biografía", "excerpt_sha256": "52bb2c635b61da1ceda607668259c7ce7ceeae98d5d41e43754693c261e0a0f1", "verification_status": "accepted", "verification_notes": []}, {"evidence_id": "BATCH-0011-E007", "source_id": "SRC-fe2838fe23fb7b11", "url": "https://elpais.com/mexico/2023-02-14/el-gobierno-de-lopez-obrador-vuelve-a-cambiar-el-tramo-5-del-tren-maya-ahora-42-kilometros-seran-elevados.html", "title": "El Gobierno vuelve a cambiar el Tramo 5 del Tren Maya", "publisher": "EL PAÍS México", "raw_sha256": "9c883d2fa5d0133558a6046451d0c2aedd0652397ead38b7bb1bee930cf09c8f", "fact_summary": "The government repeatedly changed the Tren Maya's Section 5 design, including a 42-kilometre elevated section intended to protect karst soil, cenotes, caves, and underground rivers. The changes followed tree clearing and pressure from the hotel sector; a judge suspended construction for lacking an environmental-impact statement, yet López Obrador ordered work to continue. Environmental critics reported that the revised design lacked adequate technical studies, bypassed public participation, and risked ecological damage.", "question_ids": ["4B.2", "4B.3", "4B.4", "5B.4", "8B.2", "8B.5", "8B.6", "8B.8"], "polarity": "mixed", "period_fit": "Reports design and legal decisions made during 2023, including the February announcement and subsequent construction controversy.", "ruler_attribution": "The government and López Obrador are directly linked to the design changes, continuation order, and project defense.", "limitations": ["Environmental and safety risks are partly presented through critics' assessments; the source does not establish eventual realized damage."], "start_unit": 2, "end_unit": 4, "locator": "extracted HTML block 2 through extracted HTML block 4", "exact_excerpt": "Economía\nAmérica Futura\nCiencia\nSalud\nTecnología\nCultura\nDeportes\nGente\n50 aniversario\nTren Maya\nEl Gobierno de López Obrador vuelve a cambiar el tramo 5 del Tren Maya: ahora 42 kilómetros serán elevados\nFonatur anuncia que la modificación es para proteger el delicado suelo kárstico de la zona y su sistema de cuevas y cenotes. Esta construcción no estaba contemplada en la declaración de impacto ambiental\nBeatriz Guillén\nMéxico -\n14 feb 2023 - 23:50\nCET\nCompartir en Whatsapp\nCompartir en Facebook\nCompartir en Twitter\nDesplegar Redes Sociales\nAñadir EL PAÍS en Google\nCompartir:\nWhatsapp\nFacebook\nTwitter\n\nBluesky\nLinkedin\nCopiar enlace\nZona deforestada para la construcción de las vías del tramo 5 del Tren Maya, en Playa del Carmen, el pasado 23 de marzo.\nCuartoscuro\nEl Gobierno de Andrés Manuel López Obrador ha revelado los detalles del nuevo diseño del tramo 5 del Tren Maya. En la conferencia mañanera, Javier May, director de Fonatur (Fondo Nacional de Fomento al Turismo) ha explicado que 42 kilómetros de los 67 que contempla el trozo sur, que une Playa del Carmen y Tulum, van a ser elevados. “Más del 60% de la vía será viaducto elevado para proteger el suelo kárstico, cenotes, cavernas, y ríos subterráneos de la zona”, ha dicho May, acompañado de los responsables de las empresas constructoras. Esto supone un cambio radical con respecto al plan que fue presentado en junio y cuyos impactos ambientales fueron aprobados, por lo que ahora no hay estudios sobre el efecto que va a tener la nueva construcción en una zona geológica muy delicada. Las asociaciones de medio ambiente han criticado la enésima modificación del diseño y señalan que se trata de una obra “improvisada”.\nEl tramo 5 fue desde el principio un quebradero de cabeza para la Administración de López Obrador. En el proyecto original, el trozo norte, que salía de Cancún, también iba a ser elevado y las vías iban a pasar por encima de la ciudad de Playa del Carmen, según se presentó en marzo de 2021. Pero en enero del año pasado y tras dos meses de obras, con parte del bulevar ya construido, May salió a cancelar la idea debido a la dificultad que tenía llevarla cabo, técnica y económicamente. “El Tren Maya va a ras de tierra, no se va a elevar y no se va a usar la zona urbana. Esta decisión nos permite decir que la obra se va a concluir en tiempo”, declaró al anunciar los cambios el titular de Fonatur. Esto implicaba que la obra ya no iría por encima de la carretera federal 307, sino al lado.\nUn mes más tarde,\nel trazado del tren dio un nuevo giro\n: ya no iba a ir paralelo a la carretera, sino que se iba a internar unos siete kilómetros selva adentro. El Gobierno tomó la decisión tras la presión del poderoso\nlobby\nhotelero de Riviera Maya, que se negaba a que las vías se comieran parte de las entradas a sus resorts de lujo. Tras una negociación con la asociación de hoteles y después de haber talado ya 20.000 árboles, la Administración cedió a internarse en el segundo pulmón forestal de América Latina, después de la Amazonía, donde se encontraban decenas de cenotes y cuevas protegidas.\nSin embargo, en abril\nun juez federal suspendió la construcción\nporque no contaba con un requisito imprescindible: la declaración de impacto ambiental, llamada MIA. Este documento se solicita para todas las edificaciones del país, pero el megaproyecto, que iba a transcurrir sobre el mayor sistema de cuevas sumergidas del mundo, no tenía. Pese a la suspensión, López Obrador ordenó seguir adelante. El presidente ha asegurado que toda la infraestructura del tren, va a estar terminado en diciembre de este año “aunque parezca increíble”.\nFinalmente, el Gobierno presenta ante la Secretaría de Medio Ambiente su estudio de impacto ambiental. Solo en el tramo sur se recogen 11 impactos severos y uno crítico, por la pérdida de 500 hectáreas de cobertura vegetal. En las 4.900 páginas del documento, se reconoce que la delicada morfología del terreno es uno de los principales obstáculos. El biólogo Rodrigo Medellín identificó esta zona de Quintana Roo como un queso gruyere, lleno de agujeros, por su composición kárstica, que ha permitido la creación natural de un inmenso sistema subterráneo interconectado.\nMaquinaria parada durante la construcción del tramo 5 sur del Tren Maya.\nElizabeth Ruiz\nEl estudio de impacto ambiental del Gobierno registra que este sistema kárstico\npuede verse afectado “por hundimientos y derrumbes por operación de maquinaria”\ndurante la preparación del sitio y la construcción, y también por las vibraciones que provocará el tren cuando esté operativo. Pese a esto, el documento concluye que los efectos no serán significativos. Además, explica que se trata de un proyecto que “se instalará a nivel de suelo y no requerirá de infraestructura alguna que le brinde mayor altura, con la que este podría llegar a ser más visible”. Aunque llega tarde y con la obra empezada, la declaración de impacto ambiental ayuda a desbloquear el camino judicial.\nCon este historial de idas y vueltas, en diciembre el presidente recupera la idea de hacer el tren elevado. En un video publicado en su cuenta de Twitter y frente a una maqueta, López Obrador explica “la solución” frente a la complejidad del terreno: pilotes que se hunden 25 metros por debajo del suelo y permiten elevar las vías. “A la altura de Tulum, es la zona de más riesgo kárstico que tenemos. Pero debajo de la capa de agua dulce y del nivel medio del mar, el terreno es muy consistente, muy uniforme, prácticamente no hay karsticidad, entonces la decisión fue: vámonos a 25 metros de la superficie”, explicó el coronel Carrasco de la Secretaría de la Defensa Nacional, que dijo que se habían hecho 3.600 sondeos además de pruebas de carga.\nEsa información había quedado en paréntesis hasta el anuncio de esta semana, donde el tren elevado parece ser ya una realidad. El Gobierno ha especificado que el viaducto va a tener 17,5 metros de alto y además se va a construir un puente atirantado de 260 metros, justo por encima de\nla Garra del Jaguar, un laberinto de cuevas subterráneas\n. Las tres empresas encargadas de esta parte del tramo, Mota-Engil, Grupo Indi e ICA, han afirmado en la mañanera que esta nueva idea “permite una interacción amable con el entorno geofísico, en términos de suelo”.\n\nEl problema, apunta Aaron Siller, director de la oficina sureste del Centro Mexicano de Derecho Ambiental (CEMDA), es que no se cuentan con los estudios técnicos para saber qué impacto van a tener estos gigantescos pilares en el terreno, pues que el suelo tiene una infinidad de conductos subterráneos. “Han ido modificando y modificando cada vez la ingeniería de la obra, lo que es un indicio inequívoco de que se ha violado el principio precautorio, de si no sabes con certeza técnica qué impacto vas a causar, no lo hagas. Ellos nunca han tenido certeza, por eso han modificado tanto el tramo. Además, se han hecho obras diferentes a las que se manifestaron en la MIA: se ha violado lo autorizado, igual que se está violando sistemáticamente el derecho a la participación ciudadana, porque nunca se consultó con la ciudadanía la parte de que iba a ser elevado”, señala.\nNo es la primera vez en el mundo que se construye sobre un suelo kárstico, pero, por ejemplo, en el ferrocarril que atraviesa unas de las formaciones de karst más espectaculares del planeta, en la provincia china de Guizhou, la planeación para 800 kilómetros duró ocho años y de esos estudios incluso salieron seis patentes de nuevas tecnologías para evitar la vibración en el suelo. En el caso del tramo cinco, apenas se sabe desde hace dos meses qué tipo de construcción va a ser finalmente.\nSiller hace hincapié en los efectos que tiene en el medio ambiente, todo lo que se ha iniciado y ya dejado atrás: “Desmontaron toda la selva y ahora dicen que va a ser elevado. Ha habido una serie de errores y una impunidad total. Está siendo una obra improvisada, lo que da inseguridad, de riesgos civiles o de un accidente, un derrame, que podría provocar una catástrofe ecológica”. Mientras, sin hacer oídos a las críticas, el presidente López Obrador asegura sobre su proyecto estrella: “Una obra como esta, de 1.500 kilómetros, no se está haciendo en la actualidad en ningún lugar del mundo, esto es un orgullo de nuestra ingeniería civil y militar”.\nSuscríbase aquí\na la\nnewsletter\nde EL PAÍS México y reciba todas las claves informativas de la actualidad de este país\nSobre la firma\nBeatriz Guillén\nBeatriz Guillén - twitter\nVer biografía\nReportera de EL PAÍS en México. Las declaraciones de militares del 27 batallón, tanto mandos como\nmiembros de servicios de inteligencia, han ido cambiando de versión según\nse han desarrollado las investigaciones. Ocultaron inicialmente que acudieron\na barandillas y, salvo en un caso, también ocultaron que fueron al palacio\nmunicipal en la noche. A pesar de tener la evidencia de disparos y ataque a\nlos jóvenes, señalan que no salieron del cuartel bajo el argumento que no les\ncorrespondía o que tenían información de la policía municipal de que todo estaba\ncorrecto, lo cual no es cierto. Inteligencia militar\ntuvo presencia en los dos lugares de ataques a los estudiantes y no solo en uno\ncomo se dijo, y tanto los OBI como sus responsables mintieron sobre ello o\nsobre las circunstancias de los hechos en varias ocasiones.\nExiste evidencia técnica de que varios mintieron sobre sus movimientos esa noche,\nsiendo mucho más amplios, estando en varios escenarios que no reconocieron\ny tiempo antes del que señalaron en sus declaraciones. Dichas evidencias han sido de nuevo reiteradas a la Fiscalía\nespecial UEILCA, lo que debe guiar su acción son las pruebas existentes y no\nórdenes de otra índole, tal y como se han comprometido las autoridades ante los\nfamiliares. Deben investigarse los señalamientos de autoridades del Estado, tanto\nde gobernación del Estado como de la PGJ, realizadas ante UEILCA como ante\nel GIEI, de que se habría dado una falta de colaboración del ejército (el 27 de\n7\n\nUna visión global sobre los hechos, las responsabilidades y la situación del caso Ayotzinapa\nseptiembre de 2014) para proporcionar sus instalaciones para iniciar la custodia\nde los policías municipales que participaron en el ataque a los normalistas, que\nfinalmente se dio en las instalaciones de CRAPOL, y señalaron que no solo\npor autoridades del B27, sino por órdenes la ZM35 y por indicación directa del\ncomandante de la IX Región militar. Dicha denuncia de falta de colaboración no\nha sido investigada. Además de la información sobre colusión de las autoridades\nmunicipales que se señaló que fue compartida en el Grupo de Coordinación\nGuerrero Seguro antes de los hechos, el ejército tenía meses antes su propia\ninformación sobre la colusión del presidente municipal de Iguala, Cocula y\notras poblaciones, como ha mostrado la revisión de archivos, sin que se llevara\nninguna actuación sobre ello.\n12. El ejército tenía varios soldados infiltrados en la Escuela de Ayotzinapa.\nUno de ellos era uno de los 43 jóvenes desaparecidos. A pesar de esto, se\nocultó este hecho durante años, y después se ha señalado que se encontraba\ncon licencia para estudiar y no estaba activo, pero dicha respuesta no responde\na la verdad porque hay documentación de SEDENA que muestra que fue\nasignado con función de OBI (fachada) dentro de la Escuela y por lo tanto\ninformaba permanentemente de los movimientos de los estudiantes, asambleas\ny acciones, como otros OBI en la escuela que han seguido existiendo. De todo\nesto se encontró prueba documental que contiene información precisa de las\nactividades internas de la Escuela, de la toma de buses, de las actividades\nprevistas para el mes de septiembre de 2014. Igualmente, los OBI se reportaban\ntodos los días para dar cuenta de su situación, como ha reconocido uno de\nellos ante UEILCA.\nAdemás, existe registro en archivos de SEDENA de que un OBI se reportó\nel día 27 a sus superiores, cuando ya se habían llevado a cabo los hechos y\ndetenciones, y mencionó que no se comunicaría más por seguridad. A la petición\ndel GIEI de informar sobre esta circunstancia documentada en un FCA suscrito\npor el general de la ZM35, SEDENA ha negado tener información sobre la\nidentificación de ese soldado o sobre lo que comunicó a sus superiores, respuesta\nque para el GIEI y los familiares es inaceptable con las pruebas existentes.\nOtro OBI que cumplía funciones en la Escuela, declaró ante UEILCA que él no\nfue a la movilización porque estaba en segundo año, y eso correspondía a los\nde primer curso, lo que confirma por qué se movilizaron solo los estudiantes\nde primero. Otro tercer OBI reportó ese mismo día que vio al soldado que se\nencontraba desaparecido en la escuela el día 27, y si bien puede haber una\nconfusión sobre ello, debido a la existencia de varios OBI, a la vez muestra que\nse conocía su desaparición desde ese momento.\n8\n\nA ocho años y medio del caso\nEl comandante general de SEDENA en la época, señaló en documento oficial que\nel soldado desaparecido no se había comunicado desde el día 22 de septiembre\ny que las autoridades desconocían donde estaba, cosa que no responde a la\nrealidad. Por otra parte, desde el día 27, SEDENA tomó contacto con la familia\nde dicho OBI. Todo esto fue ocultado en la investigación durante 7 años, hasta\nque el GIEI encontró los documentos en los archivos de SEDENA tras la orden\nde acceso del presidente de México.\n13. SEDENA tenía conocimiento en tiempo real de lo que estaba sucediendo\na través de interceptaciones telefónicas cuya transcripción parcial fue\nencontrada en sus archivos en documentos. Reportes existentes en los archivos\nmilitares dan cuenta de parte de conversaciones del día 26 de septiembre entre\npersonas que decían que tenían a los muchachos y el 4 de octubre de parte de\nconversaciones, entre quienes identifican como un miembro de GU y un policía,\nsobre posible destino de los normalistas. Ninguna acción en ese momento se\ndio para proteger o rescatar a los jóvenes o buscarlos a pesar de ello.
